&=-(i\lambda)\int d\eta_{1}\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{\psi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)+\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{\psi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)^{*}}{\mathcal{P}^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}_{L}}(\eta_{0})P^{-1}_{p}(\eta_{0})}+\frac{\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{\psi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},-p)+\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{\psi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},-p)^{*}}{\mathcal{P}^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}_{L}}(\eta_{0})P^{-1}_{-p}(\eta_{0})}\bigg)\\
&=-\left(\frac{2i~\mathbb{I}m~\psi^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)}{\mathcal{P}^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}_{L}}(\eta_{0})P^{-1}_{p}(\eta_{0})}+\frac{2i~\mathbb{I}m~\psi^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},-p)}{\mathcal{P}^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}_{L}}(\eta_{0})P^{-1}_{-p}(\eta_{0})}\right)\\
&=-\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},-p)\Big)~~~~\equiv~~~~-~\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\,,\end{split}

(76)

where we have used the operation
\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}
and the new quantity
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)
as

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\frac{2i~\mathbb{I}m~\psi^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)}{\mathcal{P}^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}_{L}}(\eta_{0})P^{-1}_{p}(\eta_{0})}\,.

(77)

where
\psi^{(1)}
in a contact wavefunction coefficient
12
12

12

Conventionally it is denoted by
\psi_{n}
as an
n
-point wavefunction coefficient. However, throughout the paper we will use the superscript to denote the number of sites in the bulk.
and hence the superscript denotes the number of vertices in the bulk which is one at the contact level.

Alternatively, looking at eq.(
73
), we can also define the auxiliary counterpart of the
1
-site correlator,

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p\right)
,
in terms of the in-in propagators as follows:

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p\right)=(i\lambda)\int d\eta_{1}\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)+\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\bigg)\,.

(78)

Similarly, the remaining part of the term-2, i.e.,
\eta_{2}
integration part in eq.(
68
), can be obtained by
\eta_{1}\leftrightarrow\eta_{2},\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\}\leftrightarrow\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\}
in eq.(
76
)

\begin{split}&\text{$\eta_{2}$ integration in ${\text{Disc}}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)|_{\text{term}-2}$ in eq.\eqref{definition term2 from B2 disc step1}}\\
&=(i\lambda)\int d\eta_{2}\Big(K_{p}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})-K_{p}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\Big)\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg)\\
&=-\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},-p)\Big)~~~~\equiv~~~~-~\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\,.\end{split}

(79)

Therefore eq.(
68
) can be written by multiplying eq.(
76
) and eq.(
79
) as follows

{\text{Disc}}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)\Bigg|_{\text{term-2}}=-\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)

(80)

Finally, combining eq.(
72
) and eq.(
80
), we obtain the following expression for eq.(
66
)

\begin{split}{\text{Disc}}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\Bigg(&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\\
&-\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(81)

This is one of the main results in our paper demonstrating the single-cut discontinuity rule for a
2
-site correlator involving conformally coupled and massless scalar polynomial interaction.

Figure 2:
Diagrammatic representation of the single-cut rule of a
2
-site correlator as in eq.(
81
). Red circle always denotes a correlator
\mathcal{B}
, whereas the purple circle denotes the auxiliary object
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
. The blue shaded line implies taking Disc with respect to the modulus of momentum corresponding to that line, and the yellow shaded line implies taking
\widetilde{\text{Disc}}
with respect to the modulus of momentum corresponding to that line.

With the expression in eq.(
81
) at our disposal let us comment on certain non-trivial aspects of this single-cut discontinuity relation and highlight the novelties of our result. First and foremost, the second term on the RHS of eq.(
81
) involving
\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)
is very counter-intuitive and to the best of our knowledge has not been derived before. The first term on the RHS involves
{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
, which is the standard contact correlator. The origin of the product of two contact correlators obtained through cutting the only available internal line in a
2
-site correlator is understandable, since after cutting the internal line, if we take the cut line to the boundary, we readily obtain the lower-point contact correlator, see the first term on the RHS of Fig.-
2
.
However, the second term on the RHS of eq.(
81
) is rather non-trivial because the object
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
is something which does not resemble any contact correlator. We call them auxiliary objects.

Secondly, we observe that for late–time IR-convergent, conformally coupled polynomial
\phi^{n}
interactions with even
n
, only the first term on the RHS of eq.(
81
) contributes, whereas for odd
n
the second term alone survives.

There is also an interesting aspect to this in terms of leading vs sub-leading contributions to the cosmological correlators as the late-time limit is taken, i.e.,
\eta_{0}\to 0
. In the following, we substantiate this pattern by examining the leading late–time behaviour of the one-site correlator
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}\},p)
and its auxiliary counterpart
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}\},p)
. In particular, we show that the relative contribution of these two building blocks is controlled entirely by their leading
\eta_{0}
-scaling in the limit
\eta_{0}\to 0
.
For IR-convergent conformally coupled interactions with
n\geq 4
and even
n
, the leading late–time contribution of the contact correlator
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
scales as
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{n})
. Consequently, the product of discontinuities of two such correlators behaves as

\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\Big|_{n=\text{even}}\sim\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{2n})\,.

(82)

By contrast, the auxiliary structure
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
has a leading contribution at order
\mathcal{O}\left(\eta_{0}^{n+1}\right)
implying

\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\Big|_{n=\text{even}}\sim\mathcal{O}\left(\eta_{0}^{2n+2}\right)\,.

(83)

Thus, in the late–time limit
\eta_{0}\to 0
, the correlator contribution in eq.(
82
) dominates for even
n
over the auxiliary counterparts in eq.(
83
).

Interestingly, for odd
n
, the situation reverses. In this case, the leading late–time behaviour of the contact correlator scales as
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{n+1})
, resulting

\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\Big|_{n=\text{odd}}\sim\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{2n+2})\,.

(84)

Meanwhile, the auxiliary object now has a leading behaviour of order
\mathcal{O}\left(\eta_{0}^{n}\right)
so that

\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\Big|_{n=\text{odd}}\sim\mathcal{O}\left(\eta_{0}^{2n}\right)\,.

(85)

Accordingly, in the
\eta_{0}\to 0
limit, the auxiliary terms in eq.(
85
) provides the dominant contribution for odd
n
over the contribution in eq.(
84
).
Explicit computations for both even and odd conformally coupled polynomial interactions, presented in §
6
, make these scaling arguments fully manifest.

At this point it is worth highlighting that in
MarinMacedo:2025jco
a relation equivalent to eq.(
81
) was obtained following a similar strategy. Consequently, the single-cut discontinuity relations at the tree-level have similarities. Additionally, they have also identified the contribution from the auxilary objects
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
’s, although in a different notation. However, we have been able to relate these auxiliary objects
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
’s to the imaginary parts of the contact wave-function coefficients, which is absent in
MarinMacedo:2025jco
. Most importantly, the relative dominance between
\mathcal{B}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
depending on whether we have
\phi^{n}
interactions with
n=
even or odd, which we discussed above, has been missed in
MarinMacedo:2025jco
. As a consequence,
MarinMacedo:2025jco
failed to capture the significant subtlety of the relative contribution of
\mathcal{B}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
’s to the discontinuity relation at late times when
\eta_{0}\to 0^{-}
, which we discussed above in eq.(
82
), (
83
), (
84
), (
85
)) for IR-convergent cases, i.e.,
\phi^{n}
with
n\geq 4
.

Finally, before we conclude this subsection, let us point out that the discontinuity relations for the
2
-site correlator derived in this subsection used technical machineries borrowing from the in-in formalism. However, one might wonder if we could have derived these discontinuity relations staying completely within the wave-function picture. This is very relevant since the discontinuity relations for the wave-function coefficients are already known in the literature and they are the building blocks for the cosmological correlators. In Appendix
C
, we show that one can indeed obtain the discontinuity relations, written in eq.(
197
) from wave-function picture, same as in eq.(
81
), for a
2
-site tree-level correlator focussing on conformally coupled scalar with polynomial interaction. Previously in
Goodhew:2020hob
in eq.(2.69) using the cutting rules for the wave-function coefficients a similar cutting rule for the
2
-site correlator was obtained. However, the pieces involving the auxiliary objects
\widetilde{B}
were missed, which is not a surprise since in
Goodhew:2020hob
only such cases were considered where the imaginary parts of the wave-function coefficients were absent. On the contrary, in our discontinuity relations, eq.(
81
) or eq.(
197
), we capture the
\widetilde{B}
’s consistently, and hence they are valid in more general cases
13
13

13

Let us highlight that our derivation of the
2
-site discontinuity relation using the wave-function approach in Appendix
C
is not discussed in
MarinMacedo:2025jco
, as they only focussed on in-in method machineries.
.

4.2
single-cut rule for r-site correlator

Following the steps in the previous subsection, we now generalise the result obtained in the previous subsection and derive a single-cut rule for a tree-level
r
-site correlator considering
\phi^{n}
interactions
14
14

14

The analysis in this section applies to cases with
r\geq 2
.
. The tree-level correlator with
r
-sites will have
(r-1)
internal lines where we will label the modulus of exchange momentum with
p_{1},\,p_{2},\cdots,p_{r-1}
. Our single-cut rule will be obtained by cutting the right-most internal line. Following the in-in formalism, such a correlator can be written as

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(r)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-1}\}\right)\\
&=(i\lambda)^{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\eta_{0}}\frac{d\eta_{r}}{(H\eta_{r})^{4}}\int_{-\infty}^{\eta_{0}}\frac{d\eta_{r-1}}{(H\eta_{r-1})^{4}}\bigg[\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})G^{++}_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{r-1},\eta_{r})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&\quad-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})G^{+-}_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{r-1},\eta_{r})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&\quad-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})G^{-+}_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{r-1},\eta_{r})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&\quad+\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})G^{--}_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{r-1},\eta_{r})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(86)

where
\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{\pm}
has been defined in eq.(
48
), and the argument
\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{j}}\}
in
\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{\pm}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{j}}\})
denotes that the vertex at which the bulk-to-boundary propagators are anchored in the bulk, is in the middle - the
j
-th one - in a
r
-site correlator.

Also, we have introduced a new object, namely
\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{\pm}
in eq.(
86
), which captures the collection of other
(r-2)
number of vertices from the
r
-site chain we are considering and hence is of order
\mathcal{O}\left((i\lambda)^{r-2}\right)
in the coupling, which is consistent since
\mathcal{B}^{(r)}
should be of order
\mathcal{O}\left((i\lambda)^{r}\right)
. Note that it consists of
(r-2)
number of bulk-to-bulk propagators. In particular one can define
\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{\pm}
as follows

\begin{split}&\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{\sigma\in\pm}\left(\{\eta_{1},\cdots,\eta_{r-1}\}\right)\\
&=(i\lambda)^{r-2}\int^{(r-2)}d\eta\sum_{\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r-2}\in\pm}\Bigg[\left(\prod_{j=1}^{r-2}\sigma_{j}\right)\left(\mathcal{K}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\}\right)\prod_{j=2}^{r-2}\mathcal{K}^{\sigma_{j}}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{j}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{j-1}}\}\right)\right)\times\\
&\quad\quad\left(\prod_{j=1}^{r-3}G_{p_{j}}^{\sigma_{j}\sigma_{j+1}}(\eta_{j},\eta_{j+1})\right)G_{p_{r-2}}^{\sigma_{r-2}\sigma}(\eta_{r-2},\eta_{r-1})\Bigg].\end{split}

(87)

Note that in the expressions above and in the following analysis, we have implicitly made a choice, that our single-cut discontinuity will be obtained by cutting the right-most internal line. The expressions such as in eq.(
86
) and eq.(
87
) depend on that choice of convention.

Now, we take the discontinuity of the
r
-site correlator with respect to the bulk-to-bulk energy
p_{r-1}
, and by using the propagator properties, we obtain the following

\begin{split}&\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-1}\}\right)\\
=&(i\lambda)^{2}\int(d\eta_{r})\int(d\eta_{r-1})\bigg(\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})\bigg)\times\\
&~~~~~~~~~~\frac{\bigg(K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})+K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})\bigg)}{{P_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{0})}}\times\\
&~~~~~~~~~~\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(88)

where we have used a short-hand notation such that

\int(d\eta_{r})=\int_{-\infty}^{\eta_{0}}\frac{d\eta_{r}}{(H\eta_{r})^{4}}\,,

(89)

and we will be using it in the rest of this paper as well.

We can write the term in the third line of eq.(
88
) in a similar way following eq.(
65
)

\begin{split}&K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})+K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})+K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\Big)\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})+K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})\Big)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})-K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\Big)\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})-K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})\Big)\,,\end{split}

(90)

which will help us dis-entangle the two time integrations over
\eta_{r}
and
\eta_{r-1}
.
Now substituting eq.(
90
) in eq.(
88
), we therefore obtain the following

\begin{split}{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})&={\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})\Bigg|_{\text{term}-1}\\
&+{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})\Bigg|_{\text{term}-2}\,,\end{split}

(91)

where the two terms on the RHS are given by

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})\Bigg|_{\text{term}-1}\\
&=\frac{(i\lambda)^{2}}{2P_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{0})}\int(d\eta_{r})\int(d\eta_{r-1})\bigg(\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})\bigg)\times\\
&~~~~~~~\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})+K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\Big)\times\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})+K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})\Big)\times\\
&~~~~~~~\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(92)

and

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})\Bigg|_{\text{term}-2}\\
&=\frac{(i\lambda)^{2}}{2P_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{0})}\int(d\eta_{r})\int(d\eta_{r-1})\bigg(\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})\bigg)\times\\
&~~~~~~~\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})-K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\Big)\times\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})-K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})\Big)\times\\
&~~~~~~~\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(93)

Now that the integrations over
\eta_{r}
and
\eta_{r-1}
have become independent, we can easily perform the
\eta_{r}
integral in both the above terms in eq.(
92
) and eq.(
93
), in the similar way as shown in eq.(
71
) and eq.(
79
) respectively

\begin{split}&\text{$\eta_{r}$ integration in ${\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})|_{\text{term}-1}$ in eq.\eqref{discBrTerm1}}\\
&=(i\lambda)\int(d\eta_{r})\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})+K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})\Big)\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg)\\
&=\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})-\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},-p_{r-1})~~~~\equiv~~~~\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})\,,\end{split}

(94)

and

\begin{split}&\text{$\eta_{r}$ integration in ${\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})|_{\text{term}-2}$ in eq.\eqref{discBrTerm2}}\\
&=-(i\lambda)\int(d\eta_{r})\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})-K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r})\Big)\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg)\\
&=\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},-p_{r-1})~~~~\equiv~~~~~\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})\,.\end{split}

(95)

Next, we analyze the remaining parts in both term-1 and term-2. First, the remaining part of term-1 can be written as

\begin{split}&\text{$\eta_{r-1}$ integration in ${\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})\Bigg|_{\text{term}-1}$}\\
&=(i\lambda)\int(d\eta_{r-1})~\bigg(\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})\bigg)\times\\
&\quad\quad\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})+K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\Big)\\
&=~\mathcal{B}^{(r-1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-2}\}\right)-\mathcal{B}^{(r-1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},-p_{r-1};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-2}\}\right)\\
&\equiv~\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r-1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-2}\}\right)\,.\end{split}

(96)

Combining both the parts, we now have the final expression for term-1 as follows

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})\Bigg|_{\text{term}-1}\\
&=\frac{1}{2P_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{0})}\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r-1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-2}\}\right)\times\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})\,.\end{split}

(97)

As we have seen before for the
2
-site correlator case in the previous subsection, the term-2 in eq.(
93
) for the
r
-site correlator is non-trivial to analyze. Unlike what we observed in eq.(
96
), the
\eta_{r-1}
integration in eq.(
93
) does not manifest itself in terms of any lower point correlator (i.e.,
\mathcal{B}^{(r-1)}
), which can be seen as follows

\begin{split}&\eta_{r-1}\,\text{integration in $\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}\!\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\ldots,\mathbf{k}_{R}\}\,;\,\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{r-1}\}\right)\Bigg|_{\text{term-2}}$ in eq.\eqref{discBrTerm2}}\\
&=(i\lambda)\int(d\eta_{r-1})\,\bigg(\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\,\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\,\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})\bigg)\times\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\Big(K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})-K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\Big)\\
&=(i\lambda)\int(d\eta_{r-1})\,\bigg[\bigg(-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\,\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})K_{p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\,\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})K_{p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\bigg)\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\bigg(-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\,\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})K_{-p_{r-1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\,\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})K_{-p_{r-1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{r-1})\bigg)\bigg]\,.\end{split}

(98)

Since the final expression on the RHS in eq.(
98
) cannot be written in terms of lower point
\mathcal{B}^{(m)}
, we need to define what we call the auxiliary counterparts of the correlator
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{r-1}\}\right)\\
&=(i\lambda)^{2}\int(d\eta_{r})\int(d\eta_{r-1})~\bigg[\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})G^{++}_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{r-1},\eta_{r})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&+\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})G^{+-}_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{r-1},\eta_{r})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})G^{-+}_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{r-1},\eta_{r})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&-\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r-1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\})G^{--}_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{r-1},\eta_{r})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(99)

which can be further simplified as

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\ldots,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{r-1}\}\right)=(i\lambda)\int(d\eta_{r})~\bigg[\mathcal{V}_{(r-1)}^{+}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})+\mathcal{V}_{(r-1)}^{-}\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{r}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg]\,.\end{split}

(100)

Since
\mathcal{V}_{(r-2)}^{\pm}\sim\mathcal{O}\left((i\lambda)^{r-2}\right)
, it is obvious from eq.(
99
) and eq.(
100
) that
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r)}\sim\mathcal{O}\left((i\lambda)^{r}\right)
in the coupling, which is consistent with what is expected.

Note that we had encountered similar obstacles in the previous subsection where we looked at
2
-site correlator discontinuity, see eq.(
76
). However, here for the
r
-site case, we define the auxiliary objects more generally which reduces to eq.(
76
) for
r=2
. In eq.(
99
) the auxiliary counterparts of cosmological correlators are defined using in-in propagators. However, like we did for the
2
-site case before in eq.(
76
), the auxiliary counterparts can be expressed in terms of the wave-function coefficients as follows

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1},p)=\frac{2i\,\mathbb{I}m\,\psi_{n}(\vec{k}_{1},\ldots,\vec{k}_{n-1},\vec{p})}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}2\,\mathbb{R}e\,\psi_{2}(\vec{k}_{i})\right)\,2\,\mathbb{R}e\,\psi_{2}(\vec{p})}\,,\\[6.0pt]
&\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(2)}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{2n-2};p)=\frac{2i}{\prod_{i=1}^{2n-2}2\,\mathbb{R}e\,\psi_{2}(\vec{k}_{i})}\Bigg[\mathbb{I}m\,\psi_{2n-2}(\vec{k}_{1},\ldots,\vec{k}_{2n-2};\vec{p})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{\mathbb{I}m\,\psi_{n}(\vec{k}_{1},\ldots,\vec{k}_{n-1},\vec{p})\,\mathbb{R}e\,\psi_{n}(-\vec{p},\vec{k}_{n},\ldots,\vec{k}_{2n-2})}{\mathbb{R}e\,\psi_{2}(\vec{p})}\Bigg]\,,\\
&\ldots\ldots\,.\end{split}

(101)

Now, using eq.(
99
) in eq.(
98
) we obtain

\begin{split}&\eta_{r-1}\,\text{integration in $\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}\!\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\ldots,\mathbf{k}_{R}\}\,;\,\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{r-1}\}\right)\Bigg|_{\text{term-2}}$ in eq.\eqref{discBrTerm2}}\\
&=-\bigg(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r-1)}\!\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\ldots,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1}\,;\,\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{r-2}\}\right)\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r-1)}\!\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\ldots,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},-p_{r-1}\,;\,\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{r-2}\}\right)\bigg)\\
&=-\,\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r-1)}\!\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\ldots,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1}\,;\,\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{r-2}\}\right)\,,\end{split}

(102)

Combining eq.(
95
) and eq.(
102
) we finally obtain

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})\Bigg|_{\text{term}-2}\\
&=-\frac{1}{2P_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{0})}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r-1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-2}\}\right)\times\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})\,.\end{split}

(103)

Finally, adding eq.(
97
) and eq.(
103
) we get the following discontinuity relation of a r-site correlator

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})\\
&=\frac{1}{2P_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{0})}\bigg[\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r-1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-2}\}\right)\times\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})\\
&~~-\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r-1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-2}\}\right)\times\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(104)

which is one of the main results we derive in this paper. This relation can be pictorially presented as in Fig.
3
.

Figure 3:
Diagrammatic representation of the single-cut rule of a
r
-site correlator as in eq.(
104
). Red circle denotes correlator
\mathcal{B}
, the purple circle denotes the auxiliary object
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
and the patterned blob represents
\mathcal{V}^{\sigma}_{(r-2)}
. The first term in the RHS denotes the term
\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r-1)}\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
and the second term describes the term
\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r-1)}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
as given in eq.(
104
) .

In appendix-
D
we discuss the derivation of discontinuity relation for a tree-level
3
-site correlator in detail, which might help one to understand the abstract calculations involving notational complexities performed in this section in the simplest non-trivial example beyond the
2
-site case.

We conclude this section by making some comments on the single-cut discontinuity relation in the context of the flat space cosmological correlators. It is easy to see that in the flat space, the bulk to bulk propagators satisfy the same identity as we have obtained in eq.(
60
) for the half-integer
\nu
. Therefore, it is trivially expected that the single-cut discontinuity relation we have derived in this section will work through and we have discussed this exhaustively in Appendix-
G
.

5
Generalizations to other cases

In this section, we generalise the derivation of the single-cut discontinuity relation to other cases involving massless scalar fields with interactions of the type
\chi^{n}
, interactions involving the derivative of the scalar field, and to loop diagrams beyond the tree-level.

5.1
Massless scalar

It is rather straightforward to argue that the derivation of the single-cut discontinuity relation, which so far has focused on conformally coupled scalar fields, also applies to massless interacting scalar fields. The crucial reason supporting this is to note the following property of the power spectrum for massless scalar fields, i.e.,

P_{p}(\eta_{0})=\Big|f_{p}^{+}(\eta_{0})\Big|^{2}=\frac{H^{2}}{2p^{3}}\big(1+p^{2}\eta_{0}^{2}\big)\,,~~\text{and hence}~~P_{-p}(\eta_{0})=-P_{p}(\eta_{0})\,,

(105)

which follows from its definition and the corresponding mode expansion, eq.(
4
),

\begin{split}f^{+}_{p}(\eta)=\frac{H}{\sqrt{2p^{3}}}(1-ip\eta)e^{ip\eta}\,,\quad f_{p}^{-}(\eta)=\frac{H}{\sqrt{2p^{3}}}(1+ip\eta)e^{-ip\eta}\,.\end{split}

(106)

Once eq.(
105
) is established, one can check that the calculations performed in §
3
and §
4
also apply rather straightforwardly to massless interacting scalar fields. In other words, all the identities involving in-in bulk-to-boundary and bulk-to-bulk propagators, and therefore the basic relations significant for the derivation of the single-cut discontinuity relation, hold for massless scalars as well. Consequently, the cutting rule for the two-site massless correlator with a
\chi^{n}
bulk interaction takes the same form as before. Hence, the discontinuity relation for
2
-site correlator with massless scalar fields is given by the following

\begin{split}{\text{Disc}}_{p}\mathcal{B}_{\chi^{n}}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\Bigg(&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}_{\chi^{n}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}_{\chi^{n}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\\
&-\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\chi^{n}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\chi^{n}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\Bigg)\,,\end{split}

(107)

where
\mathcal{B}_{\chi^{n}}^{(2)}
denotes the
2
-site correlator for a massless theory with
\chi^{n}
interaction and
\mathcal{B}_{\chi^{n}}^{(1)}
denotes the
n
-point contact correlator for the massless theory.

For IR-finite interactions involving massless scalars, the wave-function coefficients are purely real
Stefanyszyn:2023qov
;
Cabass_2023
. In this case, the second term on the RHS of eq.(
107
) does not contribute, and the result is entirely determined by the first term. This agrees with the result obtained in
Goodhew:2020hob
. For IR-divergent interactions, however, the wave-function coefficients of massless scalars acquire imaginary contributions. In this case, both terms on the right-hand side of eq.(
107
) contribute.

5.2
Derivative interaction

Here in this subsection, we will derive the single-cut discontinuity rule for a derivative interaction with the following interaction term in the Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}_{\text{int}}=\lambda~\phi^{n-1}\partial_{\eta}^{(s)}{\phi}\,,

(108)

where the superscript
s
indicates that the derivative is taken
s
times with respect to conformal time
\eta
.
For convenience, we treat the field
\partial_{\eta}^{(s)}{\phi}
as if it propagates through the bulk. Therefore, in this case, the in-in bulk-to-bulk propagators take the following form

\begin{split}G^{++}_{p}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})&=\frac{1}{P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\bigg[\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{(s)}K^{-}_{p}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})~\partial_{\eta_{2}}^{(s)}K^{+}_{p}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})~\theta(\eta_{1}-\eta_{2})+(\eta_{1}\leftrightarrow\eta_{2})\bigg]\,,\\
G^{--}_{p}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})&=\frac{1}{P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\bigg[\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{(s)}K^{+}_{p}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})\,\partial_{\eta_{2}}^{(s)}K^{-}_{p}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})~\theta(\eta_{1}-\eta_{2})+(\eta_{1}\leftrightarrow\eta_{2})\bigg]\,,\\
G^{+-}_{p}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})&=\frac{\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{(s)}K^{+}_{p}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})~\partial_{\eta_{2}}^{(s)}K^{-}_{p}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})}{P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\,,\\
G^{-+}_{p}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})&=\frac{\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{(s)}K^{-}_{p}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})~\partial_{\eta_{2}}^{(s)}K^{+}_{p}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})}{P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\,.\end{split}

(109)

The
2
-site correlator then has the following form by definition of the in-in formalism

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\partial_{\eta}}^{(2)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p\right)=(i\lambda)^{2}\int^{(2)}d\eta~\bigg[\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})G^{++}_{p}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&\quad\quad-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})G^{+-}_{p}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})G^{-+}_{p}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&\quad\quad+\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})G^{--}_{p}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg]\,.\end{split}

(110)

where to distinguish from the many other correlators studied in this paper, we are using the subscript
\partial_{\eta}
to remind the readers that we are considering a derivative interaction.

Now we take the discontinuity of the above expression in eq.(
110
) with respect to the bulk-to-bulk energy (denoted by
p
) followed by manipulations using the in-in propagator identities written in eq.(
60
), to get the following

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p}\mathcal{B}_{\partial_{\eta}}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=(i\lambda)^{2}\int^{(2)}d\eta~\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})\bigg)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\frac{\bigg(\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{(s)}K_{p}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})~\partial_{\eta_{2}}^{(s)}K_{p}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})+\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{(s)}K_{p}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})~\partial_{\eta_{2}}^{(s)}K_{p}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\bigg)}{{P_{p}(\eta_{0})}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(111)

The above expression is analogous to the one in eq.(
63
) except that the mode function associated with the bulk-to-bulk propagators is swapped with its time derivatives. Therefore, the subsequent derivation follows the same pattern as the non-derivative case done in §
4.1
. Therefore, following the same steps, we obtain the single-cut rule for
2
-site correlator with derivative interaction

\begin{split}{\text{Disc}}_{p}\mathcal{B}_{\partial_{\eta}}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\Bigg(&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}_{\partial_{\eta}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}_{\partial_{\eta}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\\
&-\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\partial_{\eta}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\partial\eta}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(112)

Note that here both the contact correlators
\mathcal{B}_{\partial_{\eta}}^{(1)}
and the one site auxiliary object we have defined in
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\partial_{\eta}}^{(1)}
must contain a boundary field
\partial_{\eta}^{(s)}{\phi}
along with
(n-1)
- number of
\phi(\eta_{0})
.

5.3
Loop diagrams

In this subsection, considering
\lambda\phi^{n}
interaction, we derive the discontinuity relation involving loop diagrams for the
2
-site correlator,
\mathcal{B}^{(2)}
. As we discussed in §
2
, we first define the object which appears as the the integrand in the loop integration over the momenta running over the loop as follows

\mathcal{B}^{(2)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\}\right)=\int_{\vec{p_{1}},\vec{p_{2}}}\mathbb{B}^{(2)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},p_{2}\right)\,.

(113)

Note that
\mathcal{B}^{(2)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\}\right)
is the
2
-site
1
-loop correlator, and
\mathbb{B}^{(2)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},p_{2}\right)
is the integrand in the loop integration. Also the two momenta flowing in the two sides of the loop are being denoted by
\vec{p}_{1}
and
\vec{p}_{2}
and the subscript
\vec{p}_{1},\vec{p}_{2}
in the integral in eq.(
113
) implies the loop integrals over
3
-momenta.

Using the bulk-to-boundary and bulk-to-bulk propagators in the in-in formalism, for a
2
-site
1
-loop diagram one can write

\begin{split}&\mathbb{B}^{(2)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},p_{2}\right)\\
&=(i\lambda)^{2}\int^{(2)}d\eta\bigg[\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})G^{++}_{p_{1}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})G^{++}_{p_{2}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})G^{+-}_{p_{1}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})G^{+-}_{p_{2}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})G^{-+}_{p_{1}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})G^{-+}_{p_{2}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad+\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})G^{--}_{p_{1}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})G^{--}_{p_{2}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg]\,\end{split}

(114)

where by
\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\}
we denote the collection of external momenta
\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\}\equiv\{\vec{k}_{1},\cdots,\vec{k}_{n-2}\}
and
\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\}\equiv\{\vec{k}_{n-1},\cdots,\vec{k}_{2n-4}\}
. Substituting this in eq.(
113
) will produce the full correlator for us.

As we will see, to write the discontinuity relation we will have to cut both the sides of the loop, resulting in taking successive discontinuities with respect to
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
15
15

15

One could have thought of defining an operation
\text{Disc}_{p_{1},p_{2}}
to take the discontinuity with respect to both
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
simultaneously as was done, e.g., in eq.(3.11) in
Melville:2021lst
, which we have previously mentioned as the
simultaneous-cut
. This is different from what we have employed, namely the the
successive-cuts
, where we have applied the discontinuity operation successively on all the sides of the loops.
. Let us first compute the discontinuity of the above correlator with respect to
p_{2}

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\mathbb{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},p_{2})\\
&=(i\lambda)^{2}\int^{(2)}d\eta\Bigg[\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})\Big(G^{++}_{p_{1}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-G^{+-}_{p_{1}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\Big)\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})\Big(G^{-+}_{p_{1}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-G^{--}_{p_{1}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\Big)\Bigg]\times\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~\frac{\bigg(K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})+K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\bigg)}{{P_{p_{2}}(\eta_{0})}}\,.\end{split}

(115)

Again, applying the discontinuity with respect to
p_{1}
we get the following

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{1}}\Big[{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\mathbb{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},p_{2})\Big]\\
&=\frac{(i\lambda)^{2}}{{{P_{p_{1}}(\eta_{0})}}{{P_{p_{2}}(\eta_{0})}}}\int^{(2)}d\eta~\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{1}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\})\bigg)\times\\
&\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{+}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})-\mathcal{K}_{\eta_{0}\eta_{2}}^{-}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\})\bigg)\times\bigg(K_{p_{1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})+K_{p_{1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\bigg)\times\\
&\bigg(K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})+K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(116)

To proceed further, we need to rewrite the following pieces in the above equation as follows

\begin{split}&K_{p_{1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})+K_{p_{1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Big(K_{p_{1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})+K_{p_{1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})\Big)\Big(K_{p_{1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})+K_{p_{1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\Big)\\
&~+\frac{1}{2}\Big(K_{p_{1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})-K_{p_{1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})\Big)\Big(K_{p_{1}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})-K_{p_{1}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\Big)\,,\end{split}

(117)

and, similarly,

\begin{split}&K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})+K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Big(K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})+K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})\Big)\Big(K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})+K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\Big)\\
&~+\frac{1}{2}\Big(K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})-K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})\Big)\Big(K_{p_{2}}^{+}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})-K_{p_{2}}^{-}(\eta_{0},\eta_{2})\Big)\,.\end{split}

(118)

Next we substitute eq.(
117
) and eq.(
118
) into eq.(
116
). The resulting expression produces four terms which we simplify individually. After some tedious but straightforward manipulations which we defer to Appendix
E
we obtain the final result as follows

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{1}}\Big[{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\mathbb{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},p_{2})\Big]=\frac{1}{4{{P_{p_{1}}(\eta_{0})}}{{P_{p_{2}}(\eta_{0})}}}\times\\
&\Bigg[\bigg({\text{Disc}}_{p_{1}}{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2})\times(L\to R)+\widetilde{{\text{Disc}}}_{p_{1}}\widetilde{{\text{Disc}}}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2})\times(L\to R)\bigg)\\
&-\bigg({\text{Disc}}_{p_{1}}\widetilde{{\text{Disc}}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2})\times(L\to R)+\widetilde{{\text{Disc}}}_{p_{1}}{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2})\times(L\to R)\bigg)\Bigg]\,.\end{split}

(119)

It is important to note that in eq.(
119
) on the RHS we have the integrand of the loop integration for the full correlator, however, on the RHS we get the contact correlator
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
or the contact auxiliary counterpart
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
.

In Fig.
4
we represent eq.(
119
) diagrammatically.

Figure 4:
Diagrammatic representation of single-cut rule for the loop integrand of a 1-loop
2
-site correlator. Each blue shaded line corresponds to taking Disc with respect to the modulus of momentum of that line. On the other hand, each yellow line corresponds to taking
\widetilde{\text{Disc}}
with respect to the modulus of the momentum of that line.

Motivated by specific structures in the derivation for the
1
-loop discontinuity result, we conjecture the following cutting rule for the
2
-site correlator at arbitrary
\ell
-loop order

\begin{split}&\mathrm{Disc}_{p_{\ell+1}}\cdots\mathrm{Disc}_{p_{1}}\,\mathbb{B}^{(2)}\!\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell+1}\right)=\frac{1}{2^{\ell+1}\left(\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{\ell+1}P_{p_{j}}(\eta_{0})\right)}\times\\
&\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq\{1,\dots,\ell+1\}\\
|S|\ \mathrm{even}\end{subarray}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{S}\,\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\};p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell+1})\right)\times\left(\mathcal{D}_{S}\,\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell+1})\right)\\
&\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq\{1,\dots,\ell+1\}\\
|S|\ \mathrm{odd}\end{subarray}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{S}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\};p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell+1})\right)\times\left(\mathcal{D}_{S}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell+1})\right)\Bigg]\,,\end{split}

(120)

where the operator
\mathcal{D}_{S}
(with the same subset
S
as in the sums above) is defined by

\mathcal{D}_{S}\;\equiv\;\left(\prod_{i\in S}\widetilde{\mathrm{Disc}}_{p_{i}}\right)\times\left(\prod_{i\notin S}\mathrm{Disc}_{p_{i}}\right)\,.

Here,
|S|
is just the number of elements in
S
. Each subset
S
determines which internal momenta get a
\widetilde{\mathrm{Disc}}
: if
|S|
is even,
\mathcal{D}_{S}
acts on
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
; if odd, it acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
.

It is worth noting that in our discontinuity relation for loops we have used successive-cut of the loop arms, which is different from the simultaneous-cut employed in earlier studies, although at the level of wave-function coefficients, such as
Melville:2021lst
or at the correlator level in
Donath:2024utn

16
16

16

For the loop diagrams
MarinMacedo:2025jco
employed the same simultaneous-cut for the discontinuity but we
have adopted the successive single-cut method which are different at the level of operations.
. In our opinion, the successive single cut in the case of loop diagrams are more advantageous than the simultaneous cut. One advantage is that, if we take successive cuts, the RHS in eq.(
119
) and eq.(
120
) can be obtained solely in terms of the contact level objects (both
\mathcal{B}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
) which is relatively easier to compute. However, for simultaneous cut, since one needs to take all possible combinations of the cut lines and hence need to consider tree-level diagrams (for one loop at the LHS). Now the situation becomes complicated if you start increasing the number of loops. In that case, we believe our eq.(
120
) is more elegant.

6
Explicit checks

In this section, we present several explicit examples with various interactions that demonstrate the validity and generality of the single-cut rules or the discontinuity relations derived formally in the previous section.

For concreteness, in this section we focus on conformally coupled scalar fields with IR convergent polynomial self-interactions of the type
\phi^{n}
. This allows us to make analytic progress while still capturing the structural features that are essential for the single-cut construction. In particular, to illustrate the interplay between the two types of building blocks,
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
we analyse both odd and even polynomial interactions (i.e.,
\phi^{n}
with
n=
even and
n=
odd) in the simplest non-trivial setting of a
2
-site correlator. These examples provide a clear demonstration of how the two building blocks enter differently depending on the odd and even polynomial interaction.

Additionally, we are also able to highlight how the leading and sub-leading pieces in the correlator play an essential role in making the discontinuity relation work as we take the late-time limit in the
\eta_{0}\to 0^{-}
perturbative approximation. In this context, we also identify a shortcoming of the recently proposed cutting rule in
Donath:2024utn
, providing explicit examples that clarify the situations where the formula is effective and where it fails. We also establish that our result is universally true in every example we have examined.

Furthermore, we extend the analysis to correlators with more than two sites. This allows us to substantiate a vital claim made in one of our previous sections: for odd interactions (i.e.,
\phi^{n}
with
n=
odd) with more than two sites, both the terms on the RHS in eq.(
81
):
\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
and
\widetilde{\text{Disc}_{p}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}\widetilde{\text{Disc}_{p}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
, contribute at the same order in the late-time expansion. Consequently, both must be included on equal footing to correctly capture the leading behaviour as
\eta_{0}\to 0
. The explicit examples we work through confirm this expectation and illustrate how the single-cut rule naturally incorporates both types of contributions when required.

6.1
2
-site correlator for conformally coupled interaction

6.1.1
2
-site correlator with
\phi^{5}

We first compute a
2
-site correlator for a conformally coupled
\phi^{5}
interaction using the in-in formalism. We have done the analytical computation in Mathematica, and the notebook can be found in the following link, see the file
‘Nonderivative in dS.nb’
available at -

(link)

17
17

17

The file can be accessed at
‘github.com/DKaran98/Single-cut-disc’
.
. The explicit expression of the correlator takes the following form

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{10}\eta_{0}^{8}}{64k_{1}...k_{8}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}^{2}+3k_{L}k_{R}+k_{R}^{2}+2p(k_{L}+k_{R})+p^{2}}{(p+k_{L})^{2}(p+k_{R})^{2}(k_{L}+k_{R})^{3}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(121)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}
, and
k_{R}=k_{5}+k_{6}+k_{7}+k_{8}
.
Then by taking a discontinuity with respect to the bulk-to-bulk exchange energy
p
we get the following expression

\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{10}\eta_{0}^{8}}{16k_{1}k_{2}...k_{8}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}p}{(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}}\right)\,.

(122)

To compare this with the RHS of eq.(
81
) we need to know the expression of
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\frac{2i~\mathbb{I}m~\psi^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)}{\mathcal{P}^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}_{L}}(\eta_{0})P^{-1}_{p}(\eta_{0})}=\frac{2i~\mathbb{I}m~\psi^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)}{\Big(P_{k_{1}}(\eta_{0})P_{k_{2}}(\eta_{0})P_{k_{3}}(\eta_{0})P_{k_{4}}(\eta_{0})P_{p}(\eta_{0})\Big)^{-1}}\,,\end{split}

(123)

where
P_{k}(\eta_{0})^{-1}=\frac{2k}{H^{2}\eta_{0}^{2}}
, etc.

For a
\phi^{5}
theory, a contact wave-function has the following expression at the leading order in the late time
\eta_{0}

\psi^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\frac{i\lambda}{H^{4}\eta_{0}^{5}}\frac{1}{(k_{L}+p)^{2}}\implies\mathbb{I}m~\psi^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\frac{\lambda}{H^{4}\eta_{0}^{5}}\frac{1}{(k_{L}+p)^{2}}\,.

(124)

Therefore, we can obtain

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=i\left(\frac{\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{16k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}\right)\frac{1}{p(k_{L}+p)^{2}}\,,\\
\implies&~\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\left(\frac{-i\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{4k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}\right)\frac{k_{L}}{(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(125)

Then the RHS of eq.(
81
) gives the following

\begin{split}&-\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{10}\eta_{0}^{8}}{16k_{1}k_{2}...k_{8}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}\,k_{R}\,p}{(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(126)

Comparing eq.(
126
) with eq.(
122
), we see that the discontinuity relation holds for
2
-sites tree level exchange correlator with a conformally coupled
\phi^{n}
interaction with
n=odd
, what we have chosen to be
n=5
.

Figure 5:
Diagrammatic representations of the discontinuity relation of a
2
-site correlator with
\phi^{5}
interaction in de Sitter. LHS signifies the eq.(
122
) and the RHS denotes eq.(
126
).

6.1.2
2
-site correlator with
\phi^{6}

We now consider an even polynomial interaction. Following the standard in-in prescription, a
2
-site correlator with a conformally coupled
\phi^{6}
interaction takes the following expression

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{14}\eta_{0}^{10}}{128k_{1}...k_{10}}\right)\left(\frac{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{L}+k_{R})^{5}+3(k_{L}^{4}+5k_{L}^{3}k_{R}+10k_{L}^{2}k_{R}^{2}+5k_{L}k_{R}^{3}+k_{R}^{4})p\\
+9(k_{L}+k_{R})(k_{L}^{2}+4k_{L}k_{R}+k_{R}^{2})p^{2}+(19k_{L}^{2}+50k_{L}k_{R}+19k_{R}^{2})p^{3}+18(k_{L}+k_{R})p^{4}+6p^{5}\end{subarray}}{p(k_{L}+k_{R})^{5}(k_{L}+p)^{3}(k_{R}+p)^{3}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(127)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+k_{5}
, and
k_{R}=k_{6}+k_{7}+k_{8}+k_{9}+k_{10}
. By taking the discontinuity with respect to the bulk-to-bulk exchange energy
p
the following expression can be obtained

\begin{split}&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{14}\eta_{0}^{10}}{64k_{1}k_{2}...k_{10}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}(k_{L}^{2}+3p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}+3p^{2})}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(128)

To compare this with the RHS of the discontinuity eq.(
81
) we need to know the expression of
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)
and
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)
which can be computed using the standard in-in formalism. One can show that it takes the following form

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\left(\frac{-\lambda H^{8}\eta_{0}^{6}}{16k_{1}k_{2}...k_{5}}\right)\frac{1}{p(k_{L}+p)^{3}}\\
\implies&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\left(\frac{-\lambda H^{8}\eta_{0}^{6}}{8k_{1}k_{2}...k_{5}}\right)\frac{k_{L}(k_{L}^{2}+3p^{2})}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}}\end{split}

(129)

As a result the RHS of the discontinuity equation takes the following form

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\\
&=~\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{14}\eta_{0}^{10}}{64k_{1}k_{2}...k_{10}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}(k_{L}^{2}+3p^{2})k_{R}(k_{R}^{2}+3p^{2})}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}}\right)\end{split}

(130)

By comparing eq.(
130
) with eq.(
128
) we see that the both side agrees showing that the discontinuity relation holds for a
2
-sites tree level exchange with conformally coupled
\phi^{n}
interaction with
n=even
. Here we have chosen
n
to be 6.

Figure 6:
Diagrammatic representations of the discontinuity relation of a
2
-site correlator with
\phi^{6}
interaction in de Sitter. LHS signifies the eq.(
128
) and the RHS denotes eq.(
130
).

6.2
3
-site correlator for conformally coupled interaction

6.2.1
3
-site correlator with
\phi^{5}

We now analyze a three-site correlator generated by a
\phi^{5}
interaction. This case is particularly instructive because both types of discontinuities—the correlator discontinuities
\mathcal{B}
and the auxiliary discontinuities
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
—contribute simultaneously.

The analytic computation is rather involved, so as an additional consistency check we have verified the single-cut rule numerically by fixing representative values for the external energies in Mathematica. The notebook can be found in the following link, see the file
‘Nonderivative in dS.nb’
available at -

(link)

18
18

18

The file can be accessed at
‘github.com/DKaran98/Single-cut-disc’
.
.

It can be seen from the mathematica file
‘Nonderivative in dS.nb’
that in this case, the leading
\eta_{0}
contribution from
\mathcal{B}^{(2)}
comes at
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{8})
and the same happens for
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
at order
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{6})
. Therefore, the first term in the RHS of the eq.(
104
) with
r=3
becomes

\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{M}\},p_{2};p_{1})\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{2})\sim\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{14})\,,

(131)

where
\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\}=\{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\}
,
\{\mathbf{k}_{M}\}=\{k_{5},k_{6},k_{7}\}
and
\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\}=\{k_{8},k_{9},k_{10},k_{11}\}
.

And for the other piece in the eq.(
104
) the leading order in
\eta_{0}
contribution also comes at
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{14})

\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{M}\},p_{2};p_{1})\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{2})\sim\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{14})\,.

(132)

Figure 7:
Diagrammatic representations of the single-cut discontinuity relation of a 3-site correlator with
\phi^{5}
interaction. The first term in the RHS signifies the eq.(
131
) and the second term denotes eq.(
132
)

Therefore, we need to take both the contributions into the account since they come at the same order in
\eta_{0}
.

6.2.2
3
-site correlator with
\phi^{6}

We now analyze a three-site correlator generated by a
\phi^{6}
interaction. In this case, only the correlator discontinuities
\mathcal{B}
contribute which is expected since we are now dealing with an
n=even
interaction.
The analytic computation is more involved than the previous case, so as a consistency check we have verified the single-cut rule numerically by fixing representative values for the external energies. For details please check the mathematica notebook
‘‘Nonderivative in dS.nb"
.

Figure 8:
Diagrammatic representations of a single-cut 3-site correlator with
\phi^{6}
interaction. The RHS represents the equation eq.(
133
) since it is leading in
\eta_{0}
.

In this case, we have found that the leading
\eta_{0}
contribution from
\mathcal{B}^{(2)}
comes at
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{10})
and from the contact correlator
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
the leading
\eta_{0}
contribution comes at
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{6})
. As a result the first term in eq.(
104
) with
r=3
becomes

\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{M}\},p_{2};p_{1})\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{2})\sim\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{16})\,,

(133)

where
\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\}=\{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4},k_{5}\}
,
\{\mathbf{k}_{M}\}=\{k_{6},k_{7},k_{8},k_{9}\}
and
\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\}=\{k_{10},k_{11},k_{12},k_{13},k_{14}\}
.

While for the other piece in the eq.(
215
) the leading
\eta_{0}
contribution from
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(2)}
comes at
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{11})
and from
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
the leading
\eta_{0}
contribution appears at
\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{7})
. Therefore, the other piece in eq.(
104
) becomes sub-leading in
\eta_{0}

\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(2)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{M}\},p_{2};p_{1})\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{6}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{2})\sim\mathcal{O}(\eta_{0}^{18})\,.

(134)

As a result, as long as we are concerned with the leading
\eta_{0}
behaviour in the limit
\eta_{0}\to 0
, it is enough to consider the product of the correlator discontinuity ignoring the sub-leading contribution due to the discontinuity of the auxiliary object. In the mathematica notebook
‘‘Nonderivative in dS.nb"
, it has been shown that the result is in a perfect reflection of the above argument.

6.3
2
-site correlator for conformally coupled derivative interaction

6.3.1
Conformally coupled
\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi

We are now interested in a
2
-site correlator where the at the boundary
\eta=\eta_{0}
, we have inserted the fields
\phi(\eta_{0})
(but not its derivative) and in the bulk we let the field
\partial_{\eta}\phi
propagate.

From the standard in-in prescription one can easily compute the 8-point correlator which takes the following form

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{10}\eta_{0}^{8}}{128k_{1}k_{2}...k_{8}}\right)\left(\frac{\begin{subarray}{c}2p^{5}+4p^{4}(k_{L}+k_{R})-2pk_{L}k_{R}(k_{L}+k_{R})^{2}-k_{L}k_{R}(k_{L}+k_{R})^{3}\\
+2p^{3}(k_{L}^{2}+3k_{L}k_{R}+k_{R}^{2})\end{subarray}}{p(p+k_{L})^{2}(p+k_{R})^{2}(k_{L}+k_{R})^{3}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(135)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}
, and
k_{R}=k_{5}+k_{6}+k_{7}+k_{8}
.
By taking its discontinuity with respect to the bulk-to-bulk energy we obtain the following simplified form which is essentially the LHS of the eq.(
112
)

\begin{split}&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{10}\eta_{0}^{8}}{64k_{1}k_{2}...k_{8}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}(k_{L}^{2}+p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}+p^{2})}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(136)

To compare this with the RHS of the discontinuity eq.(
112
) we need to know the expression of
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)
and
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)
which can be computed using the standard in-in formalism. One can show that it takes the following form

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\left(\frac{\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{16k_{1}...k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}}{p(k_{L}+p)^{2}}\right)\\
\implies&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\left(\frac{\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{8k_{1}...k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}(k_{L}^{2}+p^{2})}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(137)

Figure 9:
Diagrammatic representations of the discontinuity relation of a
2
-site correlator with
\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi
interaction. Wavy line denotes the field
\partial_{\eta}\phi
. The LHS denotes the eq.(
136
) and the RHS signifies the eq.(
138
).

Therefore the leading
\eta_{0}
contribution in the RHS of the eq.(
112
) becomes the following

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{10}\eta_{0}^{8}}{64k_{1}k_{2}...k_{8}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}(k_{L}^{2}+p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}+p^{2})}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(138)

which agrees with the LHS of the discontinuity equation that we have obtained in eq.(
136
).

6.3.2
Conformally coupled
\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi

Let’s now consider an interaction
\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi
involving conformally coupled fields and similar to the case formerly studied we let the field
\partial_{\eta}\phi
propagate in the bulk.
From the standard in-in prescription one can easily compute the 8-point correlator which takes the following form

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{14}\eta_{0}^{10}}{128k_{1}...k_{10}}\right)\times\mathcal{N}\,,\end{split}

(139)

such that

\begin{split}\mathcal{N}=\left(\frac{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{L}k_{R}(k_{L}+k_{R})^{2}(k_{L}^{2}+3k_{L}k_{R}+k_{R}^{2})-(k_{L}+k_{R})^{3}(k_{L}^{2}-k_{L}k_{R}+k_{R}^{2})p-(3k_{L}^{4}\\
+14k_{L}^{3}k_{R}+28k_{L}^{2}k_{R}^{2}+14k_{L}k_{R}^{3}+3k_{R}^{4})p^{2}-9(k_{L}+k_{R})(k_{L}^{2}+4k_{L}k_{R}+k_{R}^{2})p^{3}\\
-(19k_{L}^{2}+50k_{L}k_{R}+19k_{R}^{2})p^{4}-18(k_{L}+k_{R})p^{5}-6p^{6}\end{subarray}}{(k_{L}+k_{R})^{5}(k_{L}+p)^{3}(k_{R}+p)^{3}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(140)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+k_{5}
, and
k_{R}=k_{6}+k_{7}+k_{8}+k_{9}+k_{10}
.

By taking discontinuity with respect to the bulk-to-bulk energy
p
we obtain the following expression that corresponds to the LHS of the eq.(
112
)

\begin{split}&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{14}\eta_{0}^{10}}{16k_{1}...k_{10}}\right)\left(\frac{pk_{L}k_{R}(k_{L}^{2}+p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}+p^{2})}{(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(141)

To compare this with the RHS of the discontinuity eq.(
112
) we need to know the expression of
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)
which can be computed using the standard in-in formalism. One can show that it takes the following form

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=i\left(\frac{\lambda H^{8}\eta_{0}^{6}}{32k_{1}...k_{5}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}-p}{p(k_{L}+p)^{3}}\right)\\
\implies&\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=-i\left(\frac{\lambda H^{8}\eta_{0}^{6}}{4k_{1}...k_{5}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}(k_{L}^{2}+p^{2})}{(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(142)

Figure 10:
Diagrammatic representations of the discontinuity relation of a
2
-site correlator with
\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi
interaction. The LHS denotes the eq.(
141
) and the RHS signifies the eq.(
143
).

Therefore the leading
\eta_{0}
piece in the RHS of the eq.(
112
) becomes the following

\begin{split}&-\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}\partial_{\eta}\phi}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{14}\eta_{0}^{10}}{16k_{1}...k_{10}}\right)\left(\frac{pk_{L}k_{R}(k_{L}^{2}+p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}+p^{2})}{(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{3}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(143)

which agrees with the LHS of the discontinuity equation that we have obtained in eq.(
141
).

6.4
1
-loop correlator for conformally coupled interaction

One should note that for a tree level diagram it is enough to take discontinuity with respect to any one bulk-to-bulk energy to separate out the two adjacent vertices. However, in case of the loop one does not have this freedom. For example, in our current case, to separate out the two adjacent vertices one must take discontinuities with respect to modulus of both the bulk-to-bulk momenta
\vec{p}_{1}
and
\vec{p}_{2}
.
In the following we present two examples of the derivative interaction. Depending on the nature of the interaction, whether it is odd or even polynomial, the discontinuities of the correlator or the auxiliary part will contribute.
To find the detailed computation see the mathematica notebook
‘‘1 loop cut in dS.nb"
available at -

(link)

20
20

20

The file can be accessed at
‘github.com/DKaran98/Single-cut-disc’
.
.

6.4.1
2
-site
1
-loop correlator with
\phi^{4}

With a
\phi^{4}
conformally coupled interaction we study a 1-loop, 4 point correlator. Using standard in-in formalism one can find the integrand of the loop integration as follows

\begin{split}&\mathbb{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{4}\text{-loop}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1};p_{2}\})=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{4}\eta_{0}^{4}}{16k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}+k_{R}+p_{1}+p_{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}(k_{L}+k_{R})(k_{L}+p_{1}+p_{2})(k_{R}+p_{1}+p_{2})}\right)\,.\end{split}

(144)

So in principle, to obtain the 2-site correlator we indeed have to perform two three momenta integrations over
\vec{p}_{1}
and
\vec{p}_{2}
.
Taking discontinuities with respect to the magnitude of both the bulk-to-bulk momenta successively we obtain the following

\begin{split}&\text{Disc}_{p_{1}}\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathbb{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{4}\text{-loop}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1};p_{2}\})\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{4}\eta_{0}^{4}}{4k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{(k_{L}^{2}-p_{1}^{2})(k_{R}^{2}-p_{1}^{2})-(k_{L}^{2}+k_{R}^{2}-6p_{1}^{2})p_{2}^{2}+p_{2}^{4}}{p_{1}p_{2}(p_{+}^{2}-k_{L}^{2})(p_{+}^{2}-k_{R}^{2})(p_{-}^{2}-k_{L}^{2})(p_{-}^{2}-k_{R}^{2})}\right)\,,\end{split}

(145)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2},~~k_{R}=k_{3}+k_{4},\,p_{+}=p_{1}+p_{2},\,p_{-}=p_{1}-p_{2}
, and this is the LHS of the discontinuity equation.

To compute the RHS we just need the information of a one site correlator, to be precise, a 4 point contact correlator in this case. In
\lambda\phi^{4}
theory, a 4 point contact correlator is given by the following

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}}(k_{1},k_{2},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{4}}{8k_{1}k_{2}}\right)\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}(k_{1}+k_{2}+p_{1}+p_{2})}\,.\end{split}

(146)

Using this, one can find the following quantities required to compute the RHS of the Disc equation given by eq.(
119
)
21
21

21

Note that since this is an even interaction, only the contributions from the correlator
\mathcal{B}
will only contribute.

\begin{split}&\text{Disc}_{p_{1}}\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{\lambda H^{4}\eta_{0}^{4}}{2k_{1}k_{2}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}(k_{L}^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2})}{p_{1}p_{2}\left(p_{+}^{2}-k_{L}^{2}\right)\left(p_{-}^{2}-k_{L}^{2}\right)}\right)\,,\\
&\text{Disc}_{p_{1}}\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{\lambda H^{4}\eta_{0}^{4}}{2k_{3}k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{R}(k_{R}^{2}-p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2})}{p_{1}p_{2}\left(p_{+}^{2}-k_{R}^{2}\right)\left(p_{-}^{2}-k_{R}^{2}\right)}\right)\,,\\
&\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{1}}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{\lambda H^{4}\eta_{0}^{4}}{2k_{1}k_{2}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}}{\left(p_{+}^{2}-k_{L}^{2}\right)\left(p_{-}^{2}-k_{L}^{2}\right)}\right)\,,\\
&\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{1}}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\phi^{4}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{\lambda H^{4}\eta_{0}^{4}}{2k_{3}k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{R}}{\left(p_{+}^{2}-k_{R}^{2}\right)\left(p_{-}^{2}-k_{R}^{2}\right)}\right)\,.\end{split}

(147)

Substituting them in the RHS of the Disc equation it is straight forward to verify that it correctly reproduces what we obtained in the LHS.

6.4.2
2
-site
1
-loop correlator with
\phi^{5}

Using a
\phi^{5}
conformally coupled interaction we study a 1-loop, 6 point correlator. Using standard in-in formalism one can find the integrand of the loop integration as follows

\begin{split}&\mathbb{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}\text{-loop}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1};p_{2}\})\\
&=\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{8}\eta_{0}^{6}}{32k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}k_{5}k_{6}}\right)\left(\frac{(p_{1}+p_{2})(k_{L}^{2}+3k_{L}k_{R}+2k_{L}(p_{1}+p_{2})+(k_{R}+p_{1}+p_{2})^{2})}{p_{1}p_{2}(k_{L}+k_{R})^{3}(k_{L}+p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}(k_{R}+p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(148)

Now one can take the discontinuity with respect to both
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
successively by using the following definition. The result is slightly cumbersome and obtained in the mathematica notebook
‘‘1 loop cut in dS.nb"
available at -

(link)

22
22

22

The file can be accessed at
‘github.com/DKaran98/Single-cut-disc’
.
.

The next step is to check how to obtain the RHS of the Disc. equation and hence we need to know just one information that is the imaginary part of a
5
-point contact wave-function coefficient.

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},p_{1},p_{2})=&\frac{2i~\mathbb{I}m\psi^{(1)}\left(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},p_{1},p_{2}\right)}{P_{k_{1}}^{-1}(\eta_{0})P_{k_{2}}^{-1}(\eta_{0})P_{k_{3}}^{-1}(\eta_{0})P_{p_{1}}^{-1}(\eta_{0})P_{p_{1}}^{-1}(\eta_{0})}\\
=&~\left(\frac{i\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{16k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2}+k_{L})^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(149)

From the above expression one can find the following expressions which constitute the RHS of the Disc equation given by eq.(
119
)
23
23

23

Since this is an odd interaction, only the contributions from the auxiliary object
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
will only contribute.

\begin{split}&\text{Disc}_{p_{1}}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{-i\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{2k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}\left(k_{L}^{4}-3p_{1}^{4}+2p_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}+p_{2}^{4}+2k_{L}^{2}p_{-}p_{+}\right)}{p_{1}\left(p_{+}^{2}-k_{L}^{2}\right)^{2}\left(p_{-}^{2}-k_{L}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\,,\\
&\text{Disc}_{p_{1}}\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{-i\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{2k_{4}k_{5}k_{6}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{R}\left(k_{R}^{4}-3p_{1}^{4}+2p_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}+p_{2}^{4}+2k_{R}^{2}p_{-}p_{+}\right)}{p_{1}\left(p_{+}^{2}-k_{R}^{2}\right)^{2}\left(p_{-}^{2}-k_{R}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\,,\\
&\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{1}}{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{-i\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{2k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}\left((k_{L}^{2}-p_{1}^{2})^{2}+2(k_{L}^{2}+p_{1}^{2})p_{2}^{2}-3p_{2}^{4}\right)}{p_{2}\left(p_{+}^{2}-k_{L}^{2}\right)^{2}\left(p_{-}^{2}-k_{L}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\,,\\
&\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p_{1}}{\text{Disc}}_{p_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}_{\phi^{5}}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{1},p_{2})=\left(\frac{-i\lambda H^{6}\eta_{0}^{5}}{2k_{4}k_{5}k_{6}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{R}\left((k_{R}^{2}-p_{1}^{2})^{2}+2(k_{R}^{2}+p_{1}^{2})p_{2}^{2}-3p_{2}^{4}\right)}{p_{2}\left(p_{+}^{2}-k_{R}^{2}\right)^{2}\left(p_{-}^{2}-k_{R}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(150)

Substituting them in the RHS of the Disc equation it is straight forward to verify that it correctly reproduces what we obtained in the LHS.

To summarise this section, we have provided various explicit checks above in support of our discontinuity relations written explicitly in terms of the cosmological correlators, which are summarised in eq.(
81
) and eq.(
104
) for tree-level correlators, and in §
5.2
and §
5.3
for derivative intercations and loop diagrams respectively.

Before we conclude this section let us comment on how our results compare with recent results available in the literature. In particular, in
Donath:2024utn
, cutting rules for a
2
-site correlator, even with loop-diagrams was written down, using the Veltman’s largest time equation and the in-out formalism developed directly in the context of cosmological correlator. Although we followed a different method in this paper, it is certainly instructive to compare both the results.

Our attempts to understand the cutting rule written in eq.(5.32) in
Donath:2024utn
yield the following - we find that for conformally coupled
\phi^{4}
theory both at the tree-level and at
1
-loop level, eq.(5.32) in
Donath:2024utn
is consistent, However, for
\phi^{5}
interactions it is incomplete in a sense that the RHS of eq.(5.32) in
Donath:2024utn
fails to capture the leading
\eta_{0}
contribution and hence does not agree with the LHS for odd polynomial interaction, both at the tree and at the loop level. The details of this check are given in Appendix
F
. On the other hand, in previous subsections, namely in §
6.1.1
and §
6.4.2
we have checked that our formulae eq.(
81
) and eq.(
119
) work for conformally coupled scalars with
\phi^{5}
interaction, both at the tree level and at the
1
-loop level respectively. In this sense, we believe that our discontinuity relations are improvements over the existing results in the literature
24
24

24

The comparison to the discontinuity relation in
Donath:2024utn
(see the discussions above and in the Appendix
F
) is not present in
MarinMacedo:2025jco
.
.

7
Partial energy singularity: a corollary derived from discontinuity relation

In this section we argue that starting from the single-cut discontinuity relation that we derived in §
4
, one can gain further insight about the analytic structure of cosmological correlators. Particularly, our aim will be to look at what is known as the partial energy singularity of the cosmological correlators.

It is known that the cosmological wave-function coefficients exhibit two types of singularities: total energy singularities which occur when the sum of external energies or absolute values of external momenta vanishes, and partial energy singularities arising when the total energy flowing into a interaction vertex vanishes
Baumann:2020dch
;
Baumann:2021fxj
. Furthermore, the residue of the total energy singularity of a wave-function coefficient is known to give us the corresponding flat-space scattering amplitude
Raju:2012zr
;
Baumann:2020dch
;
Baumann:2021fxj
. The same statement holds for the total energy singularity of cosmological correlators
Goodhew:2020hob
. While making this statement, the choice of initial conditions as the Bunch-Davies one has been an important assumption, since otherwise one might encounter other singularities such as the folded singularities
25
25

25

Configurations where two or more momenta become collinear.
. Consequently, cosmological correlators also inherit these two types of singularities from the wave-function coefficients
Goodhew:2020hob
. In the following, we analyze the behavior of cosmological correlators at a partial energy pole.

Our strategy is to start from our single-cut discontinuity relation that we have derived for the cosmological correlators and take the limit
E_{L}\to 0
, where
E_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}+p
denotes the partial energy that flows into the left most vertex in a
r
-site correlator
\mathcal{B}^{(r)}
considering cubic interaction in the bulk. To start with, we only consider the
2
-site correlator
\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4};p)
and the corresponding discntinuity relation was derived in eq.(
81
), which we write here again for convenience

\begin{split}{\text{Disc}}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\frac{1}{2P_{p}(\eta_{0})}\Bigg(&\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\\
&-\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)\times\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(151)

Recalling the definition of
{\text{Disc}}_{p}
and
\widetilde{\text{Disc}}_{p}
from eq.(
25
) and eq.(
26
), we can now take the
E_{L}\to 0
limit on the LHS and RHS of eq.(
151
) independently and then equate them to obtain our desired result. For the LHS we get

\begin{split}\lim_{E_{L}\to 0}{\text{Disc}}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)&=\lim_{E_{L}\to 0}\left[\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4};p)-\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4};-p)\right]\\
&=\lim_{E_{L}\to 0}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4};p)\,,\end{split}

(152)

where we have used that the exchange correlator does not have a folded singularity due to our choice of Bunch-Davies initial condition. Also, note that
\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)
developes a pole as
E_{L}\to 0
, however,
\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4};-p)
remains finite, as the latter has a pole at
E_{L}-2p\to 0
