\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}\},\{p_{i}\}_{i=1}^{L+1}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\Bigg(\mathcal{B}^{(1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}\},\{p_{i}\}_{i=1}^{L+1}\right)+(-1)^{L+1}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}\},\{-p_{i}\}_{i=1}^{L+1}\right)\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(235)

Comparing with our
2
-site discontinuity relation derived in eq.(
81
) we find that at the tree-level (which corresponds to
L=0
) eq.(
234
) gives consistent result for cases with
\phi^{n}
interactions with
n=
even (i.e., in eq.(
233
), when
(m+L+1)~\text{and}~(n-m+L+1)
are both even integers). However, for odd polynomial interactions (
n=
odd with
\phi^{n}
interactions), the RHS of the eq.(
234
) fails to capture the leading
\eta_{0}
contribution as
\eta_{0}\to 0^{-}
, where
\eta_{0}
is the late time-slice in de Sitter. In other words, for the leading
\eta_{0}
behaviour the equality stops to hold. We have also checked that the situation is same at
1
-loop (which corresponds to
L=1
). In the following discussion we explicitly show this observation for both tree and
1
-loop cases using odd and even polynomial interaction.

•

For
\phi^{4}
theory at tree level:
We consider a conformally coupled
\phi^{4}
theory
35
35

35

This corresponds to
m=3
and
n=6
.
. Following the in-in formalism one can first derive a
2
-site correlator and then substitute it in the LHS of the eq.(
234
) that yields the following expression

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p\right)+(-1)^{6}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{-\mathbf{k}_{L},-\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p\right)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}(H\eta_{0})^{6}}{16k_{1}...k_{6}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})}\right)\,,\end{split}

(236)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}
, and
k_{R}=k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6}
.
To obtain the RHS of eq.(
234
) one need to know a contact correlator in this theory which can easily be computed from the standard in-in formalism. Then substituting it in eq.(
235
) one can obtain the following expression

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p\right)&=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{4}}{8k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\right)\frac{k_{L}}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})}\,,\\
\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p\right)&=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{4}}{8k_{4}k_{5}k_{6}}\right)\frac{k_{R}}{p(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})}\,.\end{split}

(237)

Using the above expressions in the RHS of eq.(
234
), one can get the following expression

\begin{split}&\frac{2~\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p\right)\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p\right)}{P_{p}(\eta_{0})}=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}(H\eta_{0})^{6}}{16k_{1}...k_{6}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})}\right)\,,\end{split}

(238)

which is in a perfect agreement with the eq.(
236
).

•

For
\phi^{5}
theory at tree level:
Let us now consider an odd polynomial interaction in the form of conformally coupled
\phi^{5}
interaction
36
36

36

It corresponds to
m=4
and
n=8
.
. Again by standard in-in computation we can find the correlator which then yields the LHS of the eq.(
234
) as follows

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p\right)+(-1)^{8}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{-\mathbf{k}_{L},-\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p\right)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{10}\eta_{0}^{8}}{16k_{1}...k_{8}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}p}{(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(239)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}
, and
k_{R}=k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6}
.
To find the RHS, one needs to find a contact correlator in this theory that can be done using the in-in formalism. Substituting this in the RHS of eq.(
235
) we obtain the following

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p\right)=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{6}}{16k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}\right)\frac{k_{L}}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})}\,,\\
&\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p\right)=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{6}}{16k_{5}k_{6}k_{7}k_{8}}\right)\frac{k_{R}}{p(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})}\,.\end{split}

(240)

Substituting this in the RHS of eq.(
234
), we obtain the following expression

\begin{split}&\frac{2~\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p\right)\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p\right)}{P_{p}(\eta_{0})}=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}(H\eta_{0})^{10}}{64k_{1}...k_{8}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})}\right)\,,\end{split}

(241)

which does not agree with eq.(
239
).
In fact, from the RHS what we have obtained is the subleading contribution to the correlator that can be verified by looking at the order of
\eta_{0}
and the order of the singularities.

•

For
\phi^{4}
theory at loop level:
We consider a
\phi^{4}
theory
37
37

37

This corresponds to
m=2,n=4
.
in which we first find the integrand of the loop integration of a
2
-site 1-loop correlator from the in-in formalism and then using it in the LHS of eq.(
234
) we get the following expression

\begin{split}&\mathbb{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{4}-\text{loop}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},p_{2}\}\right)+(-1)^{4}\mathbb{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{4}-\text{loop}}\left(\{-\mathbf{k}_{L},-\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},p_{2}\}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}(H\eta_{0})^{4}}{8k_{1}...k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}}{p_{1}p_{2}(k_{L}^{2}-p_{+}^{2})(k_{R}^{2}-p_{+}^{2})}\right)\,,\end{split}

(242)

where we define
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2},k_{R}=k_{3}+k_{4}
and
p_{+}=p_{1}+p_{2}
.
To find the RHS of eq.(
234
), we need the following building blocks which are essentially contact level correlator, what we call
1
-site correlator

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2}\right)=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{4}}{8k_{1}k_{2}}\right)\frac{k_{L}}{p_{1}p_{2}(k_{L}^{2}-p_{+}^{2})}\,,\\
&\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{1},p_{2}\right)=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{4}}{8k_{3}k_{4}}\right)\frac{k_{R}}{p_{1}p_{2}(k_{R}^{2}-p_{+}^{2})}\,.\end{split}

(243)

Substituting these expressions in the RHS of eq.(
234
), one can see the perfect agreement with the LHS computed in eq.(
242
)

\begin{split}&2\left(\frac{\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2}\right)\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{4}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{1}p_{2}\right)}{P_{p_{1}}(\eta_{0})P_{p_{2}}(\eta_{0})}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}(H\eta_{0})^{6}}{8k_{1}...k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}}{p_{1}p_{2}\left(k_{L}^{2}-p_{+}^{2}\right)\left(k_{R}^{2}-p_{+}^{2}\right)}\right)\,.\end{split}

(244)

•

For
\phi^{5}
theory at loop level:
We now consider an odd polynomial interaction in the form of
\phi^{5}
theory at
1
-loop
38
38

38

It corresponds to
m=3
and
n=6
. From the in-in formalism one can easily compute the integrand of the loop integration of the correlator. Then the LHS of the eq.(
234
) yields the expression as follows

\begin{split}&\mathbb{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},p_{2}\}\right)+(-1)^{6}\mathbb{B}^{(2)}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{-\mathbf{k}_{L},-\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},p_{2}\}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}H^{8}\eta_{0}^{6}}{8k_{1}...k_{6}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}p_{+}^{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}\left(k_{L}^{2}-p_{+}^{2}\right)^{2}\left(k_{R}^{2}-p_{+}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(245)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}
and
k_{R}=k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6}
. To find the RHS of eq.(
234
), we need to make use of the following expression

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2}\right)=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{6}}{16k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\right)\frac{k_{L}}{p_{1}p_{2}\left(k_{L}^{2}-p_{+}^{2}\right)}\,,\\
&\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{1},p_{2}\right)=\left(\frac{\lambda(H\eta_{0})^{6}}{16k_{4}k_{5}k_{6}}\right)\frac{k_{R}}{p_{1}p_{2}\left(k_{R}^{2}-p_{+}^{2}\right)}\,.\end{split}

(246)

Substituting this in the RHS of eq.(
234
) we obtain the following expression

\begin{split}&2\left(\frac{\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p_{1},p_{2}\right)\mathcal{B}^{(1),\text{cut}}_{\phi^{5}}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{1}p_{2}\right)}{P_{p_{1}}(\eta_{0})P_{p_{2}}(\eta_{0})}\right)\\
=&\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}(H\eta_{0})^{8}}{32k_{1}...k_{6}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}}{p_{1}p_{2}\left(k_{L}^{2}-p_{+}^{2}\right)\left(k_{R}^{2}-p_{+}^{2}\right)}\right)\,.\end{split}

(247)

It is easy to see that it does not match with the LHS computed in the eq.(
245
).

Appendix G
Discontinuity of flat space cosmological correlator

In this appendix we derive the discontinuity of the in-in correlator in flat Minkowski space-time. In case of flat-space the mode functions are simple as compared to the de Sitter case.
We assume that the fields are inserted at the time slice
t_{0}=0
. We will treat this time slice as future boundary analogous to the future boundary of de Sitter. We consider massless
\phi^{n}
theory with the following action

S=\int d^{3}\vec{x}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}=0}dt\left(-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}\phi\partial^{\mu}\phi+\frac{\lambda}{n!}\phi^{n}\right)\,.

(248)

The solution derived from the equation of motion for the given action yields the mode functions, which are expressed as

f_{k}^{-}(t)=\frac{e^{-ikt}}{\sqrt{2k}},~~f_{k}^{+}(t)=\frac{e^{ikt}}{\sqrt{2k}}\,.

(249)

It is manifest that the above mode functions closely resemble the conformally coupled scalar field upto an overall factor of
(-H\eta)
. However, the energy dependence of the mode function is identical to the conformally coupled case which is why the cutting rules we have derived in this paper must be valid for the flat space correlator as well.

A striking difference between the flat space and de Sitter case is rooted in the contribution from the auxiliary objects
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(r)}
. Recall that, in flat space, a contact wave-function coefficient is real. Therefore, the auxiliary object at one site
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}
vanishes in flat space. As a result, the discontinuity of a correlator can be obtained only from the discontinuity of lower site correlators. In particular, our single-cut rule in the context of the flat space correlator takes the following simple form

\begin{split}&{\text{Disc}}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{R}\};\{p_{1},...,p_{r-1}\})=\\
&\frac{1}{2P_{p_{r-1}}(\eta_{0})}\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(r-1)}\left(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},...,\mathbf{k}_{M_{r-2}}\},p_{r-1};\{p_{1},..,p_{r-2}\}\right)\text{Disc}_{p_{r-1}}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{r-1})\,,\end{split}

(250)

where, there is no contribution from the auxiliary objects
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
. In other words, the other contribution we got in the eq.(
104
) in the de Sitter context for conformally coupled and massless scalars does not participate in the flat space part.

In the following, we will work through few explicit examples of the single-cut rule for a
2
-site and
3
-site correlator using
\phi^{3}
interaction. Since in flat space, the singularity structures of a correlator is same for any polynomial massless interaction of type
\phi^{n}
, it suffices to work with the
\phi^{3}
interaction.

2
-site correlator:
In flat space, a
2
-site correlator can be computed using the standard in-in formalism. It has the following expression

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{8k_{1}...k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{p+k_{L}+k_{R}}{p(p+k_{L})(p+k_{R})(k_{L}+k_{R})}\right)\,,\end{split}

(251)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2}
, and
k_{R}=k_{3}+k_{4}
.
Taking discontinuity with respect to the bulk-to-bulk energy (
p
) yields the following expression

\text{Disc}_{p}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4k_{1}...k_{4}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{L}k_{R}}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})}\right).

(252)

To compare this with the RHS of the discontinuity eq.(
250
) for
r=2
, we need to evaluate a
1
-site correlator which is quite straight forward from the in-in prescription. Therefore, we need to use the following building blocks to find the product of discontinuity of two
1
-site correlator

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\left(\frac{\lambda}{4k_{1}k_{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{p(k_{L}+p)}\right)\,.\end{split}

(253)

Then the discontinuity of the above 1 site correlator with respect to
p
becomes the following

\begin{split}{\text{Disc}_{p}}{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p)=\left(\frac{\lambda}{2k_{1}k_{2}}\right)\frac{k_{L}}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})}\,.\end{split}

(254)

Figure 13:
Diagrammatic representations of a
2
-site correlator with
\phi^{3}
interaction

Then the RHS of the discontinuity eq.(
250
) for
r=2
gives the following

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2P_{p}}{\text{Disc}_{p}}{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L}\},p){\text{Disc}_{p}}{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p)=&\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4k_{1}...k_{4}}\right)\frac{k_{L}k_{R}}{p(k_{L}^{2}-p^{2})(k_{R}^{2}-p^{2})}\,.\end{split}

(255)

Comparing eq.(
255
) with eq.(
252
), we see a perfect agreement.

3-site correlator:
We now consider a 3-site correlator that can be computed using the standard in-in formalism. It has a slightly lengthy expression written ss follows

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(3)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{M},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1};p_{2})=\frac{\lambda^{3}}{16k_{1}...k_{5}}\times\mathcal{F}\,,\end{split}

(256)

where
k_{L}=k_{1}+k_{2},\,k_{M}=k_{3}
and
k_{R}=k_{4}+k_{5}
and

\mathcal{F}=\frac{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{M}+k_{R}+p_{1})(k_{M}+k_{R}+p_{2})(k_{M}+p_{1}+p_{2})+k_{L}(k_{M}+k_{R}+p_{1}+p_{2})(k_{L}+2k_{M}+k_{R}+p_{1}+p_{2})\end{subarray}}{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}p_{2}(k_{L}+k_{M}+k_{R})(k_{L}+p_{1})(k_{M}+k_{R}+p_{1})(k_{L}+k_{M}+p_{2})(k_{R}+p_{2})(k_{M}+p_{1}+p_{2})\end{subarray}}\,.

(257)

Taking discontinuity with respect to one of the the bulk-to-bulk energy
p_{2}
yields the following expression

\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(3)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{M},\mathbf{k}_{R}\};p_{1};p_{2})=\frac{\lambda^{3}}{8k_{1}...k_{5}}\times\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{F}\,,

(258)

such that

\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{F}=\frac{k_{R}\Big((k_{L}+k_{M})(k_{M}+p_{1})(k_{L}+k_{M}+p_{1})-k_{M}p_{2}^{2}\Big)}{p_{1}p_{2}(k_{L}+p_{1})\left((k_{L}+k_{M})^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)\left((k_{M}+p_{1})^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)(k_{R}^{2}-p_{2}^{2})}\,.

(259)

To compare this with the RHS of the eq.(
250
) for
r=3
, we need to evaluate a
1
-site and a
2
-site correlator which is quite straight forward from the in-in prescription. Therefore, we need to use the following building blocks to find the product of discontinuity of the
2
-site and a
1
-site correlator

Figure 14:
Diagrammatic representations of a 3-site correlator with
\phi^{3}
interaction

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{M}\},p_{2};p_{1})=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{8k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\,\frac{p_{1}+k_{L}+k_{M}+p_{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+k_{L})(p_{1}+k_{M}+p_{2})(k_{L}+k_{M}+p_{2})}\,,\\
&{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{2})=\frac{\lambda}{4k_{4}k_{5}}\,\frac{1}{p_{2}(k_{R}+p_{2})}\,.\end{split}

(260)

Now the discontinuity of both the correlators with respect to
p_{2}
has the following expression

\begin{split}&\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{(2)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{L},\mathbf{k}_{M}\},p_{2};p_{1})=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\,\frac{(k_{L}+k_{M})(k_{M}+p_{1})(k_{L}+k_{M}+p_{1})-k_{M}p_{2}^{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}(k_{L}+p_{1})\left((k_{L}+k_{M})^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)\left((k_{M}+p_{1})^{2}-p_{2}^{2}\right)}\,,\\
&\text{Disc}_{p_{2}}{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)}(\{\mathbf{k}_{R}\},p_{2})=\frac{\lambda}{2k_{4}k_{5}}\,\frac{k_{R}}{p_{2}(k_{R}^{2}-p_{2}^{2})}\,.\end{split}

(261)

Now substituting the above two expressions in the RHS of the eq.(
250
) it is simple to see that we indeed get the same expression as computed in eq.(
258
).
